        notes: I've made a second fix in `outline.rs`, which I think is the actual reason `inline_reads_as_source` still fails. Nothing was built or run: the panic message was cut off again, and it isn't in `rhipper.md` or `var/runs` either. So the cause below comes from reading the code against the disassembly. The orchestrator's `cargo test -q` will show whether it holds.\n\n**Likely cause.** `matches` paired each loop block's phis by position. The lift orders a block's phis by register (`rax`, `rcx`, `rdx`, …), and the copies keep the same value in different registers:\n- **64-bit:** `show_low` and `show_high` keep `total` in `eax`; `show_from` keeps it in `edx`.\n- **32-bit:** `show_low` keeps `total` in `eax`; `show_from` keeps it in `edx` and the character in `eax`.\n\nSo `total` was paired with the string pointer `s` and the match failed. Only `show_low` and `show_high` became calls to the helper, which fits the earlier "mentioned three times" failure. The fix to the guard sharing the loop's exit, from the last run, was needed but not enough on its own.\n\n**Changed in `crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/outline.rs` (`matches`):**\n- Each loop block's ordinary instructions and its final branch are still paired in order. The phis are left for a second step.\n- The region's output values are paired with each other before the phis.\n- Each phi is then paired with whichever copy phi its uses have already been matched to. This repeats until nothing more can be paired, because one phi can feed another.\n- Any phi still unpaired after that is paired in order, as before. A phi that points to the wrong copy, or to one already taken, rejects the match.\n\n**Checked by reading:**\n- Every phi in these loops (`total`, `s`, the character) is used by the loop body, so it gets paired before the phi step.\n- The constant 100 kept in a register (`rdx` in `show_low`, `rax` in `show_from`) leaves a phi that only feeds itself. `dce::prune_trivial_phis` turns it into the constant, so the phi counts match.\n- The non-phi instructions come from the same machine code in program order, so pairing them in order is sound.\n- The borrows look right: `pair_instr` takes the match as an argument rather than capturing it, and the phi slices only borrow the two functions.\n\n**Still not done:** rejoining hot and cold halves, and loops the compiler peeled or unrolled. The guard that skips the loop also still stays in each caller rather than moving into the helper.\n\nThe `unused_mut` warning in the output is at `crates/ra-asm-emit/src/layout.rs:240`, another card's file, so I left it.\n\n## Faster next time\n- The handoff's output stopped before the panic message for the second run in a row, and the message isn't stored anywhere in the worktree; carrying the panic line would have named the failing assertion straight away.\n- [machine] The worktree has no `target/debug/ras.exe`, so the raised IR couldn't be dumped to compare the three loops; one IR dump would have shown the phi-order mismatch in a single step.

The old rules for a single value and for raw bytes are unchanged.\n- `crates/ra-asm-emit/src/rust.rs`:\n  - That local is now declared `let mut x: [T; n]`, and its loads and stores are plain `x[k]`, with no runtime call.\n  - A data-section access whose one computed index is scaled by the element's size is now `sec.at::<T>(base, index)` / `sec.put::<T>(base, index, v)` instead of the hand-built byte offset.\n  - The `unsafe` block in `main` now has a comment saying why it is there.\n  - The module doc now describes what the backend actually emits.\n- `runtime/rhipper.rs`: `Section::at` and `Section::put` are safe accessors built on `get`/`set`. The address wraps exactly as the old offset did, and the bound is still the whole section, not the array, because the machine can read past an array into what follows.\n- `crates/ra-asm-emit/tests/oracle.rs`: `control64_rust_is_safe_where_provable` now also requires every `unsafe` block in the function text to state its reason. It accepts either the indexed or the byte-offset form for the sorted `table` store, because I can't tell from reading which form the lift produces there.\n\n**Answers to the review:**\n1. **No diff:** the earlier work (`safety.rs`, the checked section accesses, safe `extern "C" fn`s, the "under half" test) reached main before base 4278e40. That is why it can't show against that base. Each of these needs its own card.\n3. **Every remaining `unsafe` block names why:** this was already true for function bodies (the two places that write `unsafe` both add the reason). It is now also true for `main`, and the oracle test enforces it.\n4. **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/mod.rs`:** still unchanged. By reading, I used `is_multiple_of` and `unwrap_or_else` where pedantic lints would otherwise fire. The orchestrator's lint is the actual proof.\n\n**What I checked by reading:**\n- An array element is always in bounds: the analysis already rejects any local access past the local's size.\n- `at`/`put` produce exactly the same offset as the old `place_offset` expression, including wraparound and negative indexes, which still stop the port.\n- The `zero(elem)` used to fill a new array is `Copy` for every plain type, so the array initializer compiles.\n\nThe check will show whether the control port still has under half the raw port's `unsafe` blocks, and whether every oracle stays green with the array locals.\n\n## Faster next time\n- [machine] My first PowerShell call failed with "pwsh timed out after 5000ms"; Bash worked straight away.\n- The review counted against a base that already contained the card's earlier work; a card note saying what of the card was already on main would have saved working that out from `git log`.

        notes: I fixed the likely causes of all three failures, but the check is the proof: I didn't build, and the error text of both failures was cut off in what reached me.\n\n**`rhtest_core_o2_runs_as_rust` and `rhtest_core_od_runs_as_rust`: one clippy error each, probably `clippy::manual_range_patterns`.** Both runs failed on a single error I couldn't see. The best match is `fallthrough` in `core.c`: its chain `case 4: case 5: case 6:` came out as `4u32 | 5u32 | 6u32 =>`, and clippy wants three or more consecutive values written as a range. The core switch test spelled out exactly that arm, which supports the guess.\n- **Fix:** in `crates/ra-asm-emit/src/rust.rs`, a new `Emitter::pattern` writes the case values in ascending order, with no repeats. A run of three or more consecutive values becomes a range such as `4u32..=6u32`; a `bool` never does, since it has no range pattern. Both the match arms and the `if matches!(…)` tests inside a chain now use it. The unused `constant` closure in `switch` is gone.\n- **Test:** `rhtest_core_switches_read_as_source` in `crates/ra-asm-emit/tests/oracle.rs` now expects `"4u32..=6u32 => {"`.\n- The sorting changes the order of values in no other test's expectation: the structure tests use `1u32 | 2u32` and `vowels`' values are already ascending.\n\n**`control64_rust_is_safe_where_provable`: an out-of-date assertion, also a guess.** The failure printed the whole proven port, which is how this test's own assertions report. I believe it is `contains("let mut local_m18: u64 = 0u64;")`. The previous round's pruning now drops a local that nothing reads, and slot -0x18 is stored and never read, so both its declaration and its store go.\n- **Fix:** the assertion now checks what the proof is about. The raw port gives the slot storage of its own (`local_m18_mem`), the proven port never does, and if the proven port declares the slot at all it is the plain `let mut local_m18: u64 = 0u64;`.\n- **Assumption:** that the raw port still declares `local_m18_mem`. Without the proofs every local gets storage, and its store reads the pointer, so pruning keeps it. If the check shows a different assertion failing, this guess was wrong.\n\n**Checked by reading:**\n- In `pattern`, after sorting and removing repeats, `values[end] + 1` can't overflow because a larger value follows it.\n- The cast style matches `int_constant`.\n\n**The review's points:**\n- **`clippy::all`:** not allowed any more. The clippy lints still allowed are listed one by one in `LINT_ALLOWS`, with the reason above them.\n- **`unused_variables`, `unused_mut`, `unused_parens`, crate-wide `dead_code`:** removed. The Rust side has its own cleanup now:\n  - `prune_locals` drops locals nothing reads.\n  - A parameter the body never reads is named with a leading `_`.\n  - `mut` appears only on a local the body assigns.\n  - `drop_empty_branches` removes the branches pruning leaves empty.\n  - `dead_code` stays only on the module lines, because every function of the image is ported whether the entry reaches it or not.\n  - `unused_assignments` is still allowed for the whole crate, because a local starts at zero and may be written before it is read.\n- **`clashing_extern_declarations`:** still an allow, limited to `mod rhipper` and `mod data`, with the reason written beside it. A program's import is declared in machine words, while the runtime declares the few it calls itself by their C signature, so the two can't agree. Making them agree would need one shared declaration, which is a card of its own if the reviewer wants it.\n\nThe JSON the workbench reads is unchanged.\n\n## Faster next time\n- Both failures reached me cut off ("… 33 more lines in the notes"), so I had to work out the lint from the fixture's source. Putting every `error:`/`warning:` line and every assertion message in the rerun prompt would point straight at the cause.\n- [machine] A failing port's scratch directory is deleted unless `RA_KEEP_SCRATCH` is set. Setting it in the orchestrator's check would let a rerun read the exact line clippy names.

I finished the pipeline and the function table, and wrote the serve side and the test.\n\n**What changed**\n- `apps/ras/src/annotations.rs`: one file per function at `rhipper/annotations/<addr>.json`, one key per line, so two people's edits merge as text and a conflict stays in one function's file. Added `annotate` (load the files and apply the names; the pipeline called it but it didn't exist) and `stamp` (the files and their change times, so the server can tell when a checkout or merge changes them).\n- `apps/ras/src/pipeline.rs`: the lift and emit steps apply the names and takeovers, and the emit step passes the annotations on to the function table.\n- `apps/ras/src/emit.rs`: `Options` carries the annotations, and `translate` puts each function's annotation into the table. `ras emit --project DIR` reads them.\n- `crates/ra-asm-emit/src/functions.rs`: `FunctionInfo` has three new fields, `annotated`, `ty` and `comment`. A name that is not a C identifier, is reserved, is already another function's, or is for a function the host's C runtime supplies is refused with error -32602. The server writes the file, answers, then reloads so later answers use the new name.\n  - New method `annotations`: lists every annotation, with warnings for files it could not read.\n  - When the files change on disk, the server reloads and then answers the request it was holding.\n- `apps/ras/src/project.rs`: a doc line on where the annotations live.\n- `apps/ras/tests/serve.rs`: new test `a_rename_through_the_server_lands_in_the_sidecar_and_the_next_emit`. If the function is later renamed through an annotation, that entry stops matching and the emit warns. Moving that method to the annotation's `hand` key, which is stored by address, would fix it; that could be a follow-up card.\n\n## Faster next time\n- A WIP commit from an earlier session was already in the worktree, and the card didn't say so. One line naming it (and that `annotate` in the pipeline was missing) would have saved the time spent finding where to start.

        objective: Two images matched function by function (the names where both have them, else the structure of the CFG, the called functions, the strings and the constants, in rounds that use the matches of the last), with the changed functions ranked by how much changed and their raised IR diffed; `ras diff a.exe b.exe` prints the report and `ras serve` answers it for the workbench's diff view; a patch to a game is explained as the functions it touched.
        acceptance: `cargo test -p ras -q` passes with the `-O2` and `-Od` builds of rhtest `core` matched by name-free structure for every function the maps agree on.
        notes: I made three changes for the last four failures, but couldn't run the test, so whether the check now passes is unknown until the orchestrator runs `cargo test -p ras -q`. The first fix is confirmed against the disassembly; the other two rest on what I expect the code to look like.\n\nThe check came back with 4 of 121 functions unmatched, down from about 18: `__RTC_Initialize`, `__RTC_Terminate`, `_items_check` and `_put_u`.\n\n- **`_items_check`** — confirmed with `llvm-objdump`. In the optimized build it addresses `0x4040f0`, the start of the `g_items` table. That table's first word is a pointer (`0x00404138`), whose bytes read as the text `8A@`. So the function got a fake string feature that the other build lacks, and the two builds never agreed. In `crates/ra-asm-analysis/src/lib.rs`, `strings_of` now skips any target whose pointer-sized word is an address inside the image.\n- **`_put_u`** — expected from the code, not checked against the binary. The optimized build inlines `put_i` into `check`. That leaves the run `[put_i]` in the old build facing `[put_u]` in the new one, so `put_i` takes `put_u`'s place. In `crates/ra-asm-analysis/src/matching.rs`, two places in a run now pair only when both, or neither, still call a function that isn't matched yet (new free function `open`). `put_i` still calls the unmatched `put_u` and the new `put_u` doesn't, so the run stays open. The step added last session, which compares how many functions each one calls and is called by, should then pair the two `put_u`s.\n- **`__RTC_Initialize` / `__RTC_Terminate`** — assumed from what I remember of the C runtime's source. The two functions have the same code, so shape alone never singles one out. The runtime should call `_RTC_Initialize` and only pass `_RTC_Terminate`'s address to `atexit`. `matching.rs` now records, per function, which kinds of origin found it (new `found` map, `origin_kind`), and runs a second shape step keyed by shape, number of callers and those kinds. If both functions are reached the same way, this step won't separate them.\n\n**Checked by reading:**\n- `Image::pointer` returns a `Result<u64>`, so `is_ok_and` works.\n- The match in `origin_kind` lists all ten variants of `Origin`.\n- The bit-mask fold infers `u16` from the map.\n- The `fits` closure in `align_callees` only borrows `self` after `unswap` has finished with `gb`.\n- I updated the module doc to cover the new shape step.\n\n**Wider effect:** the `strings_of` change reaches every consumer of `Function::strings`, including `functions.json` and the workbench. A pointer that read as text no longer shows up there as a string literal; real literals are unaffected.\n\n## Faster next time\n- [machine] Piping the output through `tail` is allowed but a leading `cd` is refused, and `llvm-objdump -s` ignores `--start-address`. A section dump through `tail -n N | head` is how to read data bytes.\n- The failure list only gives names. Disassembling the two builds' functions with `llvm-objdump --start-address` straight away is what found the `8A@` pointer.

    - 127 ‹task› Plugins over the engine's API {c4} [accepted]
        objective: The engine's API (the image, the functions, the IR, the facts, the emit) exposed to WebAssembly plugins with a stable interface: a plugin can add a name source, a raise pass, a signature library, an output language or a device model for the platform runtime, loaded by `ras` from the project or the user's plugin directory and listed by the workbench; the first plugin is one of the engine's own passes moved out, as the proof.
        acceptance: `cargo test -p ras -q` passes with a plugin that names functions from a text file loading and its names reaching `functions.json`.
        notes: I wrote the WebAssembly plugin host and the name-source plugin path, but nothing has been compiled or run: the hook refuses cargo builds in this project. The name source is the only kind that actually runs; the other four are recognised and listed but have no place in the engine yet.\n\n**State at start:** a WIP commit `b2c46aa` had the wasm decoder (`crates/ra-asm-wasm/src/decode.rs`), the C name-source plugin and its compiled `plugins/names/names.wasm`. The crate had no `lib.rs`, so it didn't build, and nothing in `ras` used it.\n\n**What changed**\n- **`crates/ra-asm-wasm/src/lib.rs`** (new): the public interface — value and function types, an error type, the host function type, and an `Instance` that sets up memory, table and globals, runs the start function and calls exports. A plugin's memory is capped at 4096 pages (256 MiB) and each call has a budget of 1e9 instructions.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-wasm/src/exec.rs`** (new): the interpreter. It covers every opcode the decoder produces, including all numeric ops with the spec's division and conversion traps. Call depth is limited to 400. A broken or hostile module gets an error, not a panic. There is a unit test for the edge cases.\n- **`apps/ras/src/plugins.rs`** (new): plugins live in `<project>/rhipper/plugins/<name>/<name>.wasm`, or in the user's directory (`RHIPPER_PLUGINS`, else `%APPDATA%/rhipper/plugins`). A project plugin hides a user plugin of the same name.\n  - The plugin interface (ABI 1) is the one `names.c` already implements: `ra_abi`, `ra_alloc`, the kind exports `ra_names`/`ra_raise`/`ra_signatures`/`ra_emit`/`ra_device`, and an import `ra.log`. The request is the plugin's files followed by one line per function.\n  - Each name a plugin returns gets the same checks as an annotation's name (`annotations::name_fault`). A name that fails, a plugin that doesn't load, and anything the plugin logs all become warnings.\n- **`apps/ras/src/annotations.rs`**: `annotate` applies the plugin names first, then the annotations, so an annotation's name wins. That covers the pipeline's lift and emit steps and `ras serve`.\n- **`apps/ras/src/emit.rs`**: `emit --project` now goes through `annotate`, so it gets the plugin names too.\n- **`apps/ras/src/main.rs`**: new command `ras plugins [--project DIR] [--json]`.\n- **`apps/ras/src/serve.rs`**: new method `plugins`.\n- **Manifests**: added `ra-asm-wasm` to the root `Cargo.toml` and `apps/ras/Cargo.toml`. It's a path crate only, with no new crates.io dependency; `Cargo.lock` will gain its entry on the first build.\n- **`apps/ras/tests/plugins.rs`** (new), the acceptance test:\n  1. It copies `names.wasm` and a `names.txt` (CRLF, with a comment, a valid line, an address with no function, and a junk line) into a scratch project, with the user plugin directory pointed at an empty folder.\n  2. It checks that `ras plugins --json` lists the plugin as kind `names` with no error.\n  3. It checks that `pipeline --only emit` puts `fib` at `0x140001330` in `build/functions.json` and in the source.\n  4. It checks that `emit --project` gives the same table and both plugin warnings.\n\n**For the workbench:** `ras serve` has a new `plugins` method returning `{abi, user, plugins: [{name, origin, dir, kinds, error}]}`, and plugin warnings appear in the `warnings` of `annotations`. `functions.json` has the same shape; a plugin's name shows as `name`, with `annotated` staying false.\n\n**Checked by reading only:**\n- The plugin binary uses 41 distinct instructions (from `llvm-objdump`), and the interpreter covers all of them.\n- The export section's size matches `memory`, `ra_abi`, `ra_alloc` and `ra_names`.\n- The request format and the warning texts match `names.c`.\n- The borrows in `invoke` only touch separate fields of the instance (the stack against memory, globals, table and the host functions).\n\n`cargo fmt` ran on both crates.         objective: The port under the host's debugger (the workbench's CLion-derived debugger over LLDB) and the reference under an emulator with a debug protocol (PCSX2, DuckStation, or the native tracer for a Windows program), stepped in lockstep by function from the recorded trace of task 110, with the registers and memory of both shown side by side and the divergence stopped at; the workbench's end-game view.
        acceptance: `cargo test -p ras -q` passes with the lockstep protocol driving the interpreter and a port of control64 to the same breakpoint with equal states.
        notes: I've changed the lockstep's comparison to fix the likely cause, but I haven't built or run anything, and I couldn't see the panic text because the check output stopped before it.\n\n**Why I think it failed.** The lift never gives a parameter a pointer type: every integer, a pointer included, is a 64-bit integer until a later pass narrows it (`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/abi.rs`, `Kind::ty`). So in control64, `print_str(s)` reaches the lockstep as a plain integer argument (`u`). The port holds the string at its own address and the interpreter holds it at another, so the two states differ. The first two steps of the test still pass: the start function has no arguments and `fib(15)` is a real integer. But `print_str("A")` comes soon after `fib`, so the "run to the first divergence" step stops there instead of reaching the end. That is the `end[side]["ended"] == true` assertion. The existing trace comparison never caught this because it doesn't compare arguments.\n\n**What changed:**\n- `apps/ras/src/lockstep.rs`:\n  - Each argument now records whether the reference holds it as an address. That is true for a non-zero integer, of pointer width, that lies inside one of the interpreter's own memory regions (image, globals, frames, stack, heap, or a host page the program touched). The check looks only at regions already registered, so it changes nothing in the interpreter's memory.\n  - When the comparison sees such an argument, it ignores the value unless the port passes a null there. This matches how real pointers were already treated.\n  - `Lockstep::start` now takes the image and reads the DLL flag and the pointer width from it. The module comment explains the rule.\n- `apps/ras/src/serve.rs`: the call passes `self.image`, and the doc for `lockstep.start` names the new field.\n- **JSON change for the workbench:** each argument in `lockstep.*` answers now has `"address": bool`. `../rhipper-ide` and its `docs/rhipper/rhipper-asm.md` should follow.\n\n**Checked by reading:** `Memory::regions()` and `Region`'s `start`/`len` are public, `Memory` is already imported in `lockstep.rs`, and `Image::pointer_size()` is used the same way in `pipeline.rs`. Nothing else calls `Lockstep::start`. Small integers like `fib`'s `0xf` fall in no region, so they are still compared exactly.\n\n**What the check will prove:** `cargo test -p ras -q` will show whether this was the actual failure. If it fails again, the panic message for `the_lockstep_stops_the_port_and_the_reference_at_the_same_entry` will name the assertion; the answer to `lockstep.step` with divergence includes `differs`, which names the function and argument where the two sides parted.\n\n## Faster next time\n- The check's output was cut before the panic message, so I had to work out the cause from the code; including the lines after `--- FAILED` would have named it at once.\n- [machine] This worktree has no `target/debug/ras.exe`, and the check's log under `var/` was refused ("you haven't granted it yet"), so I couldn't run a lockstep session to see the `differs` text.

The checks for no panics, no crashes and a successful exit are unchanged.\n- `apps/ras/src/main.rs` and `apps/ras/src/smoke.rs`: unchanged since the last run. They keep `--budget`, the smallest-first order and the `skipped` status.\n\nA file that hits the 5 s limit is reported as a timeout, which the acceptance doesn't count as a failure since only panics are. In a debug build, though, a timed-out file may stop before its later stages, so a panic there could go unseen. Nothing has been compiled or run here, because builds are refused in this session. The regression tests from the first fuzzing run, and the hardening they test, were already on the branch from the two checkpoint commits (76b2a01, 8783c29).\n\n**Already on the branch (checkpoints):**\n- **Hardening:** overflows, oversized allocations and runaway walks are now refusals or warnings that name the offset. This covers `crates/ra-asm-format/src/reader.rs` (`cstr`, `Cursor::skip`), the PE loader (the image base must stay below the top of the address space, export counts, export ordinals, relocations, the resource tree's budget, the debug and exception directories, ELF objects, XBE) and the decoders (`decode.rs` and `Arch::decode_all` wrap the address at the top of the space).\n- **Regression tests:** `crates/ra-asm-pe/tests/fuzz_regressions.rs` (11 tests), `crates/ra-asm-x86/src/decode/tests.rs` (wrap at `u64::MAX`) and `crates/ra-asm-format/src/reader/tests.rs`.\n\n**Added this session:**\n- **`fuzz/Cargo.toml`:** a separate workspace (its own `[workspace]`), so the root `crates/*` build and its `Cargo.lock` never pull in libFuzzer. The header comment gives the local run command.\n- **`fuzz/.gitignore`:** ignores the build output, corpus, crash files, seeds and the fuzz crate's lockfile.\n- **`fuzz/src/lib.rs`:**\n  - `walk` reads the parts of a loaded image that the loader leaves to its callers: the string table, the `.res` file, the C++ frames, and the x64 or ARM64 unwind data of each exception-table entry.\n  - `split` turns a decoder's input into a mode byte, an 8-byte start address and the bytes.\n- **Targets in `fuzz/fuzz_targets/`:**\n  - `pe.rs` loads any bytes as a PE.\n  - `xbe.rs` and `elf.rs` force the format's magic onto the first four bytes, so every input reaches that reader.\n  - `object.rs` covers COFF, ELF and archives.\n  - `x86.rs` picks 32- or 64-bit from the first byte and runs both decoding paths.\n  - `arm64.rs` decodes word by word and as a run.\n- **`.github/workflows/fuzz.yml` (new):** runs each target on Linux, nightly and on request, for 600 s by default. Each night starts from the corpus the earlier nights grew (kept in the Actions cache). The seeds are `testdata/bin` plus small ELF and COFF objects (x86, x64, arm64) and an `ar` archive, compiled in the job because the fixtures contain no relocatable object. A crash uploads its input.\n- **`.github/workflows/release.yml`:** a new `fuzz-targets` job runs `cargo fuzz build` on nightly, and `release` now needs it.\n\n**Checked by reading only:**\n- The calls exist and are public with the signatures used: `xbe::MAGIC`, `object::parse`, `object::parse_object`, `RuntimeFunction::unwind_info`, `RuntimeFunction::arm64_unwind`, `Arm64Unwind::operations`, `UnwindInfo::operations`, `UnwindInfo::frame_size`, `Arm64::decode_full(self)`, `X86::decode_all_full`, `Instr::end`, `indirect_operand`, `absolute_memory_operand`.\n- `split_at_checked` and `<[u8; 8]>::try_from(&[u8])` are stable APIs.\n\nThe orchestrator's `cargo test -p ra-asm-pe -p ra-asm-x86 -q` will show whether the regression tests pass. It will not build the fuzz targets: they need `libfuzzer-sys`, which is not in the root `Cargo.lock` and cannot be fetched offline. They will build for the first time in the release's `fuzz-targets` job, so the "fuzz targets build" criterion is unproven until that job runs; for the same reason the linter cannot check the `fuzz/` files here.\n\n**Gaps:**\n- There is no Mach-O loader in the engine and no console format other than XBE, so those containers have no target. A Mach-O target becomes a card once the loader exists.\n- Crashes found later have to be turned into regression tests by hand.\n\nNo JSON the workbench reads has changed.\n\n## Faster next time\n- The two WIP checkpoint commits had already done the hardening and the regression tests, and the card didn't say so. One line naming them would have saved the turns spent finding where to start.

Each review point:\n\n1. **`obfusc64_runs_lifted`**: the last check ran `cargo test -p ra-asm-lift -q`, the test is not ignored, and the check passed, so the test passed there. That covers both `run_interpreted` and `run_oracle_as`.\n\n2. **The three emit tests** are not changed on this branch because they are already marked `#[ignore]` on main, in `crates/ra-asm-emit/tests/oracle.rs`, by commit `06f307f` ("Switch fall-through as fall-through"). I checked each reason names the right card:\n   - `inline_reads_as_source`: "#116: the outlined helper is not yet called from each of the three callers"\n   - `obfusc64_runs_as_c`: "#111: the rebuilt graph of the flattened function does not run yet as 64-bit C"\n   - `obfusc_reads_as_source`: "#111: the flattened function does not yet read as its loop"\n\n   No other ignored test in the workspace belongs to this card. The rest name card 54, task 49, milestone I, or are maintenance or debugging aids.\n\n3. **`the_tests_leave_nothing_under_temp`**: this change is on `card/139` from an earlier checkpoint (`1ba637c`) and shows in `git diff main`. It was outside the card's listed files, which is why the review missed it:\n   - In `crates/ra-asm-testing/src/lib.rs`, scratch directories are now named `<temp>/<suite>/<build>-<pid>-<name>`, where `<build>` is a hash of the folder the test binary sits in. `Scratch::foreign` now counts only directories with this build's prefix that belong to another process, so other checkouts' runs going on at the same time are never counted.\n   - `apps/ras/tests/pipeline.rs` only changes that test's doc comment to say so.\n\n4. **The "promote block-name fix"** is in `crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/promote.rs`, not `raise/mod.rs`. When a call splits a block, each later piece now gets a name of its own (`<block>.<piece id>`), so two blocks no longer share a label. `raise/mod.rs` has no change; it already runs `flatten::run` at line 245, so the flatten pass is on.\n\n5. **The flatten fix** is in `crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/flatten.rs`. When the pass changed a function and a block branches back to the first block, `free_entry` puts a new entry block (`flat.entry`) in front of it. That block takes the old entry's allocations and branches to the old entry. Every join at the old entry gets an `undef` value from the new block. LLVM rejected exactly this case ("Entry block to function must not have predecessors!") in `classes32_runs_lifted` and `classes64_runs_lifted`, and both pass in the last check.\n\n6. **Whole-workspace run**: still not shown. The last check ran only `-p ra-asm-lift` and `-p ra-asm-emit`. Adding them to the card's file list would have prevented points 3 and 4.

      - 156 ‹task› A comparison fails in one line: the digest of ra-asm-testing {c1} [accepted]
          objective: The oracles print both whole outputs on a difference and the check's log is cut before the line that matters. One helper gives the first differing line with three lines of context in at most 200 characters, writes both sides under out/failed/<test>/, appends a line to out/failed/digest.txt, and compares with line endings normalized.
          acceptance: `cargo test -q -p ra-asm-testing --test digest` prints `test result: ok` with at least 5 tests: a CRLF and an LF text are equal, a difference at line 400 is reported as line 400, the message is one line under 200 characters, the two files and the digest line exist.
          notes: review by claude-opus-5-5: accepted\n\nI added the one-line comparison helper and its 5 tests. Nothing has been built or run yet: they were checked by reading only, and your check is what will compile and run them.\n\n**Changes**\n- **`crates/ra-asm-testing/src/digest.rs`** (new):\n  - `normalize` turns CRLF and a lone CR into LF before comparing.\n  - `first_difference` finds the first differing line, numbered from 1, plus up to three lines before it. A side that ends early reads as `<end>`, so a missing final newline counts as a difference.\n  - `digest_line` builds the one-line message (`<test>: line N: expected … got … after c1 | c2 | c3`) and clips it to fewer than 200 characters (`LIMIT`). The expected and actual sides keep their room before the context lines are cut.\n  - `compare(test, expected, actual)` works under the workspace's `out/failed`, and `compare_in(root, …)` does the same under any directory. On a difference they write `<root>/<test>/expected.txt` and `actual.txt`, append the message to `<root>/digest.txt` and return it as the error. On a match they remove whatever an earlier failure of that test left.\n  - In the directory name, any character other than a letter, digit, `-`, `_` or `.` becomes `_`, so `oracle::hello` becomes `oracle__hello`.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-testing/src/lib.rs`**: adds `pub mod digest;`.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-testing/Cargo.toml`**: the description now mentions the digest as well as the scratch guard.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-testing/tests/digest.rs`** (new), 5 tests. Each writes into its own `Scratch`, so the real `out/failed` is never touched:\n  - a CRLF text equals its LF twin, and nothing is written;\n  - a difference at line 400 is reported as line 400, with lines 397–399 as context and `line 400:` in the message;\n  - a message built from a 1000-character line and a long test name stays under 200 characters with no line break;\n  - a missing final line is reported as `<end>`;\n  - both side files and two digest lines are written, and a later passing comparison removes that test's directory.\n\n**What I checked by reading:** the `Result` chain in `compare_in`; that `&Scratch` converts to `&Path` through `Deref`; that comparing a `Vec<String>` with a `[&str; 3]` works; and the line arithmetic in the missing-last-line test. I ran rustfmt with width 140 on the two new files. The check (`cargo test -q -p ra-asm-testing --test digest`) will confirm the compile and the 5 tests.\n\nThe oracles do not call the helper yet; switching them over is a follow-up. No JSON that the workbench reads has changed.\n\n## Commit\nAdd a one-line digest for failed output comparisons\n\nThe oracles print both whole outputs on a difference, and a cut log hides\nthe line that matters. compare() names the first differing line and the\nthree before it in under 200 characters, ignores line endings, and writes\nboth sides and a digest line under out/failed.

A caller writes `let Some(clang) = tool("clang") else { return };`.\n  - `skip_allowed()` reads the variable; any value other than empty or `0` counts as set.\n  - `find_in(name, path)` searches any PATH-shaped list. Nothing was built or run; `rustfmt --check` (width 140) passes on the file.\n\n**Changes, all in `crates/ra-asm-emit/tests/oracle.rs`**\n- **Comparisons:** `Fixture::compare` goes through the digest (`compare_output` / `assert_output`, as in the lift oracle). It prints the exit code, stderr and symbolized crash site on stderr, then panics with the digest line alone. The three `*_expected_is_the_native_output` tests use the same path as `<binary>: native`.\n- **Test names:** `port_test` gives `<fixture> (<language>, <tag>)`, for example `hello64.exe (C, one)`, `classes64.exe (C++, cpp)`, `control64.exe (Rust, 4)`. The tag keeps two ports of one fixture in one language apart under `out/failed`; for Rust it is the functions-per-file count.\n- **Builds:** `assert_built` replaces the three build asserts (clang, `cargo clippy`, `cargo build`). On failure, `build_failure` writes the whole stderr to `out/failed/<test>/build.txt`, appends its line to `digest.txt`, and panics with `<test>: <tool>: <line>`. A build that succeeds removes a stale `build.txt`.\n- **Which line:** the message carries the first stderr line containing `error`, not the literal first line, because cargo's first line is `Compiling …` and clang's can be `In file included from …`. It falls back to the first non-empty line, strips the scratch directory prefix, and is cut under `LIMIT`.\n- **Tools:** `clang_available` and `cargo_available` are gone; the gates are `ra_asm_testing::tool("clang")` and `tool("cargo")`. It covers:\n  - the CRLF/LF equality and the exact digest line for `hello64.exe (C, one)`, with both sides on disk;\n  - a clang stderr whose first error is on its second line, giving exactly `hello64.exe (C, one): clang: program.h:7:3: error: unknown type name 'uint99_t'`, with `build.txt` and both lines in `digest.txt`;\n  - an over-long cargo line with no `error`, cut to `LIMIT - 1` characters ending in `…`.\n\n**Checked by reading:** the expected strings and directory names (`hello64.exe__C__one_`, `control64.exe__Rust__4_`) against `digest_line`, `first_difference` and `file_name`; the `&Scratch` to `&Path` coercions; that `std::io::Write as _` inside `build_failure` does not clash with the file's `std::fmt::Write`.           notes: The observer is in place; nothing was compiled or run here (builds are refused), so the check is the first compile and the first run of the tests.\n\n## What changed\n\n- `crates/ra-asm-ir/src/interp/observe.rs` (new): the `Observer` trait with `enter(&mut self, function: &str, depth: usize)` and `leave(...)`, and five tests under `interp::observe::tests`:\n  - three functions report their calls in order with depths;\n  - a host call is reported;\n  - a host callback into the module nests inside its host call;\n  - a call that stops still leaves;\n  - a run without an observer gives the same result, steps and `seen.entered` as an observed one.\n- `crates/ra-asm-ir/src/interp/mod.rs`:\n  - `pub mod observe`, `pub use observe::Observer`, and a public `Vm::observer: Option<Box<dyn Observer>>` field, `None` by default.\n  - `run_function` reports entry after the record and pause hooks and exit after the frame is released.\n  - A new private `call_host` wraps both `host.call` sites (direct call to an undefined name, indirect call to an external) with entry and exit.\n  - A private `hosted` counter tracks open host calls, so a callback's depth sits below its host call without touching `depth` or the `MAX_DEPTH` limit.\n\n## Behaviour to know\n\n- Depth is the number of calls open around this one: 0 for the run's entry, and a host call counts as a level.\n- `leave` fires on every exit, including a `Stop`, so enter and leave always balance. The record (`< name`) still omits non-returning functions as before.\n- Intrinsics (`llvm.*`) are not reported; they are answered inside the interpreter, not by the host.\n- Without an observer the cost is one `Option` test per call.\n- No JSON of `ras serve`, `functions.json` or `rhipper.json` changes.\n\n## Verified by reading\n\n- Borrows: the observer is borrowed through `self.observer` while the name comes from the module or an owned clone and the depth is copied out first.\n- The tests need no `place()` or memory: `call_named` and direct calls resolve by name, and an undefined non-`llvm.` name falls through `intrinsic` to the host.\n- Expected values and sequences were traced by hand (4, 11, 2, 14; 12 lines in the last test).\n- `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` is clean on both files.\n\n## What the check will prove\n\n`cargo test -q -p ra-asm-ir --lib interp::observe` compiles the change and runs the five tests.           notes: `Memory` now logs first reads and writes while armed. Every other accessor (`read_u32`, `fill`, `read_cstr` and the rest) goes through these two.\n- **Tests** are in a `#[cfg(test)] mod armed` with five cases: the four the card names, plus one showing a first read keeps its original bytes over a later write.\n\n## Behaviour to know\n\n- The log is kept per byte, so a read that straddles written and unwritten bytes logs only the unwritten ones.\n- Adjacent reads join into one run as well as overlapping ones.\n- An armed access costs a map operation per byte; unarmed, the cost is one `Option` test per access.\n- Only accesses made through `Memory` are seen: a host DLL writing through the program's pointers is not in the log.\n- No JSON of `ras serve`, `functions.json` or `rhipper.json` changes.\n\n## Verified by reading\n\n- `Memory` is only built through `Default`, so the new field breaks no constructor.\n- The tests use a 16-byte `Vec` as the region, kept alive for the whole test; expected values were traced by hand.\n- `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` is clean on the file.\n\n## What the check will prove\n\n`cargo test -q -p ra-asm-ir --lib interp::memory::armed` compiles the change and runs the five tests.       - 197 ‹task› The checker of the machine level {c2} [accepted]
          objective: An invariant that is code: every block ends in a terminator, every register access is a field the state's layout names, every call is direct to a lifted function or through `ra_call`, nothing is undef. `check(function, Level::Machine)` returns the first violation with its block.
          acceptance: `cargo test -q -p ra-asm-lift --test levels machine` prints `test result: ok` with at least 5 tests: a lifted fixture passes, and four broken functions written as text each fail with their own violation.
          notes: The machine-level checker is written but not compiled or run here (builds are refused in this session). The check will be the first compile and the first run of the tests.\n\n## What changed\n\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/check.rs`** (new): `check(function, Level::Machine)` returns `Result<(), Violation>`, where `Violation` carries the block's name, a `Rule` (`Terminator`, `Register`, `Call`, `Undef`) and a readable `what`. It reports the first violation in block order, then instruction order. `check` uses the x86 `%State`; `check_with` takes the layout's fields for the ARM states.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/mod.rs`**: `pub mod check;`.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/tests/levels.rs`** (new): 8 tests, all named `machine_*`.\n  - One lifts `hello64.exe` and `hello32.exe` and checks every function.\n  - One checks that a well-formed text function passes.\n  - Six broken text functions fail with their own rule and block: no terminator, slot 16 of the registers, a load straight through `%state`, a call through a pointer, a direct call without the state, a stored `undef`.\n\n## How the rules are read\n\n- **Terminator**: the IR cannot hold a block without a terminator, because the builder starts each block with `unreachable`. So a block never ended is an `unreachable` that follows no call; successors must also be blocks of the function. An empty `unreachable` block counts as ended when every predecessor has a call, which is the path past a no-return call in a throwing program.\n- **Register**: the state parameter is only the base of a `%State` address or a call argument. The indices must be the constants `0, field[, slot]` within the layout: a slot the array has, or nothing past a scalar field.\n- **Call**: no call through a pointer. `ra_call` takes the caller's state and the target. `ra_*` and `llvm.*` are free. An `imp_X` wrapper may call `X`. Any other direct call must be `void (ptr)` with the caller's state. This is judged by shape, not by name, because `check` gets only the function and `apply_names` renames lifted functions.\n- **Undef**: no operand is `undef`, except the second vector of a shuffle whose mask takes every lane from the first. The lift writes `pshufd`, `pshuflw`, `pmuludq` and `pmaddwd` that way.\n\n## Decisions and open ends\n\n- The parser refuses function bodies, so the test file carries its own small reader for the machine subset (`getelementptr`, `load`, `store`, `call`, `ret void`, `br label`, `unreachable`), built on `FunctionBuilder`.\n- `Level::Promoted` and `Level::Abi` return `Ok(())`: no rule is written for them yet, and the module doc says so.\n- Only the two hello fixtures are checked. `throws64.exe` and the ARM lifts are not; the exception-handling blocks are where the terminator rule is most likely to need widening.\n\n## Verified\n\n- By reading: every use of the state in `state.rs`, `x86.rs`, `arm64.rs`, `thumb.rs` and `lib.rs` is one of the three field addresses or a call argument. Every direct call the lift emits fits the call rule, and every `Unreachable` it sets on the hello paths follows a call.\n- `rustfmt --check` is clean on both new files.\n- The check will prove that it compiles and that the two hello fixtures hold no shape I missed.\n\n## Faster next time\n\n- The card says "written as text" but `parse.rs` refuses a function body; naming that, or allowing a reader in the test, would have saved the search.\n- The card's file list omitted `state.rs`, `x86.rs` (the call and shuffle sites) and `ra-asm-ir`'s `inst.rs`, `module.rs` and `builder.rs`, which define what the checker judges.\n- The signature `check(function, level)` carries neither module nor target; saying whether lifted callees are known by name or by shape, and which state layout applies, would have saved the design detour.\n\n## Commit\n\n```\nAdd the machine level's invariant checker\n\ncheck(function, Level::Machine) returns the first violation with its\nblock: a block never ended, a state access outside the layout, a call\nthat is neither a lifted function's nor ra_call's, an undef operand.\nTests lift the hello fixtures and break text functions one rule each.\n```
          files: crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/check.rs crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/mod.rs crates/ra-asm-lift/tests/levels.rs
          session: 9af0ff4f-2d4f-49ad-96d2-6d9dbe108196 d9b71715dd57af82 1794601216
      - 198 ‹task› The checkers of the promoted and ABI levels {c2} [accepted]
          objective: Promoted: no load or store of a register field outside the synchronization points. ABI: no state parameter, every direct call matches its callee's signature, every stack access is a local or is flagged. The flagged count is returned, since the grade counts it.
          acceptance: `cargo test -q -p ra-asm-lift --test levels raised` prints `test result: ok` with at least 8 tests (`raised_promoted_*` and `raised_abi_*`: a fixture passes at each level, and three broken functions per level each fail with their own violation).
          notes: The promoted and ABI checkers are written, but nothing was compiled or run here (builds are refused in this session). The check will be the first compile and the first run of the `raised` tests.\n\n## What changed\n\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/check.rs`**: `check` and `check_with` now return `Result<usize, Violation>`, the count of flagged stack accesses (always 0 below the ABI level). New `check_in(module, function, level)` takes the state layout from the module's `%State` and gives the ABI level its callees. `Rule` gains `Sync`, `State`, `Signature` and `Stack`.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/tests/levels.rs`**: 10 `raised_*` tests, 5 per level. The text reader gains `alloca` and `ret <ty> <value>`. The machine tests follow the new return type (`Ok(0)`), and the machine fixture test now goes through `check_in`.\n\n## How the rules are read\n\n- **Promoted** runs the machine rules first, since the `%State` interface stays, then `Sync` on the general registers and flags:\n  - a register is loaded at most once per block between two calls that take the state, and never after the block stored it;\n  - a register store sits in the run of stores right before a state call, a `ra_trap`/`ra_unsupported`, or the block's `ret`;\n  - a flag is never stored.\n  - The vector registers and `rip` stay in memory and are not judged.\n- **ABI**:\n  - `State`: no parameter named `state` and no `getelementptr` of `%State`.\n  - `Signature`: a direct call's argument types and result match the function or declaration it names, with extra arguments only for a variadic callee. A callee the module does not have is a violation. This needs the module, so `check` alone skips it and only `check_in` judges calls.\n  - `Stack`: the frame is the function's `[n x i8]` alloca. Every load or store through an address derived from it is flagged and counted; one at a known constant offset that falls outside the alloca is the violation. An access through a local's own alloca is not counted.\n\n## Decisions and open ends\n\n- The out-of-frame violation is my reading of "is a local or is flagged": the card names no failing case for the stack, and this is the one that would be memory-unsafe in the output.\n- `raised_abi_fixtures_pass` asserts that the flagged count of `hello64.exe` and `hello32.exe` is above zero, because `buf` and `written` have their addresses passed to `WriteFile`. If the frame pass handles them differently than I read, that assertion fails rather than the checker.\n- The stub of an import the host lacks is capped at eight parameters, while a call to an import with more passes its full arity. That would be a `Signature` violation; the hello fixtures have no such import.\n- Only the two hello fixtures are checked at the raised levels.\n\n## Verified\n\n- By reading `promote.rs`: in machine mode every register store comes from `flush`, which sits right before a state call, a no-return call or the `ret`; every load is a lazy first read or a predecessor's tail load.\n- By reading `promote.rs`, `stubs.rs`, `widths.rs` and `lib.rs`: lifted calls, import calls, universal stubs and the width pass keep call sites and callee types in step.\n- `rustfmt --check --edition 2024` is clean on both files.\n- The check will prove that it compiles and that the hello fixtures hold no shape I missed at either level.\n\n## Faster next time\n\n- The card's file list omitted `promote.rs` (`flush`, `load_slot`, `lower_call`), `liveness.rs` (`call_kind`, `slot_pointers`) and `frame.rs`, which define the synchronization points and the frame.\n- "Flagged" is defined nowhere in the code; one line saying which stack access is a violation and which is only counted would have saved the design detour.\n- `check(function, level)` cannot see callees; saying up front that the ABI rule may take the module would have saved weighing the signature change.\n\n## Commit\n\n```\nAdd the promoted and ABI levels' invariant checkers\n\nPromoted keeps the machine rules and lets registers meet the state\nonly at calls, stops and returns. ABI refuses the state, judges direct\ncalls against the module, and counts the accesses left in the frame.\ncheck returns that count; check_in takes the module.\n```

    - 200 ‹section› The structured tree runs {c3} [defined]
      - 201 ‹task› The interpreter runs one block and reports its exit {c2} [accepted]
          objective: The structured tree is statements over the IR's blocks. To execute a tree, the Vm must run the instructions of one block in a frame and say how it would leave (the branch taken, the switch value, a return) without following it.
          acceptance: `cargo test -q -p ra-asm-ir --lib interp::block` prints `test result: ok` with at least 4 tests: a conditional branch, a switch, a return with a value, a call inside the block.
          notes: The Vm can now run one block in a frame and report its exit without following it. Nothing was compiled or run here (builds are refused in this project); both files pass `rustfmt --check`.\n\n## What changed\n\n- **`crates/ra-asm-ir/src/interp/block.rs`** (new):\n  - `Frame<'m>` holds one activation's values, the block it ran last (which the next block's phis read from), and the frames-region mark.\n  - `Exit` is `Jump(target)`, `Branch { taken, target }`, `Switch { value, target }` or `Return(value)`, with `Exit::target()`.\n  - `Vm::frame(name, args)` opens a frame with the arguments adapted as a call adapts them. It is not a call: nothing is recorded, traced or counted in the depth.\n  - `Vm::run_block(host, frame, block)` runs the block's phis and instructions, evaluates the terminator and returns the `Exit`.\n  - `Vm::release(frame)` gives back the frame's `alloca`s.\n  - Six tests under `interp::block::tests`: the four the card names (conditional branch, switch, return with a value, call inside the block, to a module function and to the host), plus a phi reading from the block run last and an unknown block or function faulting.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-ir/src/interp/mod.rs`**:\n  - `run_blocks` is now a loop over `run_block`, so a function's own run and a tree's run share one implementation of block execution. The body moved verbatim, with the same step counting, limit, `site`, fault messages and addresses.\n  - `run_function` opens a `Frame` instead of a bare value vector.\n  - The argument adaptation of `invoke` moved into a private `adapt`, which `frame` shares.\n  - `pub mod block;` and `pub use block::{Exit, Frame};` were added; the `Terminator` import left with the moved code.\n\n## Verified by reading\n\n- `block.rs` is a child module of `interp`, so it reaches `Vm`'s private fields and the private `eval`, `exec` and `Stop::at`. `Frame`'s fields are `pub(super)` so `mod.rs` can reach them.\n- The expected values in the tests were worked through by hand, including the step counts (1 after the branch test's entry block, 2 after the call test's).\n- One difference from before: the entry block is now found through the id map rather than as index 0. The two agree unless a function has duplicate block ids.\n\n## What the check will prove\n\n- `cargo test -q -p ra-asm-ir --lib interp::block` compiles and passes the six tests.\n- The oracles of `ra-asm-lift`, which run through `run_blocks`, stay green over the refactor.\n\n## Faster next time\n\n- [machine] The Edit tool dropped a trailing space at the end of a `new_string`, which cost one more edit and format check; end a replacement on a non-space character.\n\n## Commit\n\n```\nRun one block of a function in a frame and report its exit\n\nVm::run_block executes a block's phis and instructions in a Frame and\nreturns an Exit (jump, branch, switch or return) without following it.\nrun_blocks is now a loop over it, so a function's run and a structured\ntree's run share one implementation.\n```

      - 202 ‹task› An interpreter for the structured tree {c3} [accepted]
          objective: Structuring is checked today only by compiling its output. An interpreter for `Stmt` and `Cond` (blocks, copies, moves, if, loop, break, continue, goto, switch, return) over the Vm's block runner executes a function's tree directly, so a structuring bug shows in milliseconds and names the function.
          acceptance: `cargo test -q -p ra-asm-emit --test tree` prints `test result: ok` with at least 8 tests: one small function per statement kind, each run as a graph and as a tree with the same result.
          notes: The tree interpreter and its eleven tests are written, but nothing was compiled or run (builds are refused here); all three files pass `rustfmt --check`.\n\n## What changed\n\n- **`crates/ra-asm-emit/src/tree.rs`** (new): `run_tree(vm, host, name, &Structured, args)` opens a frame with `Vm::frame`, walks the statements, releases the frame and returns the value coerced to the function's return type, as a call does.\n  - **Blocks and copies** run through `Vm::run_block`. The block before must lead there (the entry when none ran), otherwise the run faults.\n  - **Conditions** read the branch of the block run last. A later leaf of a folded `&&`/`||` chain runs the block that branch went to, so the chain walks the graph's condition blocks in order. The operand must be that block's own branch condition.\n  - **`Moves`** checks that the block it names is the one that ran last, which is where the next block's phis read from.\n  - **`Switch`** must match the last block's switch (type and operand), picks the case at the type's width, falls back to the default, and follows `falls_through`.\n  - **`Loop`, `Break`, `Continue`**: `break` and `continue` act on the innermost `Loop`, through any `switch` between. A round that runs no block is a fault.\n  - **`Goto`** needs the block in `labels` and resumes at its `Stmt::Block`, entering an `if`, loop or case from outside if needed.\n  - **`Return`** must be the last block's return; `Unreachable` is a fault.\n  - Every fault reads `the tree of @<function>: …` and names blocks as `%name`.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-emit/src/lib.rs`**: `pub mod tree;` and one line in the crate doc.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-emit/tests/tree.rs`** (new): eleven tests. Ten cover block, return, if, loop, break, continue, switch (with a fall-through and a `-1` case), moves, copy and goto. Each builds a memory-free function with `FunctionBuilder`, structures it with the real `structure`, asserts the statement kind is in the tree, then compares `call_named` with `run_tree` on several arguments. The eleventh swaps the arms of an `if` and expects a fault naming `@diamond` and `%els`.\n\n## Verified by reading\n\n- Every API the interpreter calls was checked against its definition for signature and visibility: `Vm::frame`, `run_block`, `release`, `layout().bits`, `Value::coerce`, `Function::block`, `Exit::target`, and `PartialEq` on `Operand`, `Type`, `Terminator`.\n- The structurer was traced by hand on each test function to confirm it produces the asserted kind. The copy and goto functions are phi-based versions of `two_exits_to_one_return` from `structure.rs`, whose 1-copy and 1-goto outcomes its own tests already assert.\n- Expected results were worked out by hand for every argument.\n\n## What the check will prove\n\n- `cargo test -q -p ra-asm-emit --test tree` compiles and passes the eleven tests.\n- The riskiest assertions are the kind checks (`Moves`, `Copy`, `Goto`), since they depend on my hand trace of the structurer.\n\n## Open end\n\n- The interpreter takes `Stmt::Break` as leaving the loop, as `structure.rs` defines it. `c.rs` prints it as a bare `break;`, which inside a `switch` case inside a loop would leave only the `switch` in C. I did not check whether the structurer can produce that shape; it may deserve a card.\n\n## Faster next time\n\n- The card could say that `Vm::eval` and `Frame`'s fields are private to `ra-asm-ir`, so a tree can only read block exits; settling that design took most of the reading.\n- A loop phi needs a value defined after it and `FunctionBuilder` has no way to add an incoming later; the tests patch the finished `Function`. A `FunctionBuilder::add_incoming` would remove that helper.\n\n## Commit\n\n```\nRun a function's structured tree on the IR interpreter\n\nrun_tree walks Stmt and Cond over Vm::run_block and checks each\nstatement against the exit its block reports, so a tree that leaves\nits graph faults with the function's name. Tests run one function\nper statement kind as a graph and as a tree.\n```

      - 217 ‹task› The uncovered-instruction census {c2} [accepted]
          objective: Which traps matter is a count, not a guess: `ras smoke <directory> --census` lifts every image and prints the mnemonics the lift traps on, by number of functions affected, as a pending list `ras cards` reads. Run over testdata, System32 and the person's program, it orders the lift's remaining work.
          acceptance: `cargo test -q -p ras --test census` prints `test result: ok` with at least 2 tests: over testdata/bin the census is a sorted list whose total equals the sum of the functions' trap counts.
          notes: `ras smoke <directory> --census` is written and the new test file is in place; nothing was compiled or run here, so the check is the first build of it.\n\n## What changed\n- `apps/ras/src/smoke.rs`: `Options.census` and a `census()` printer. It prints two `#` comment lines, then one line per mnemonic: `<mnemonic>: <n> functions, <n> traps, <n> files, first <addr> in <file>`. Lines are ordered by functions affected, then traps, then name. The JSON report gains `functions` on each `coverage.uncovered` entry and a new `coverage.function_traps`, the sum of the per-function trap counts of every file.\n- `crates/ra-asm-lift/src/lib.rs`: `Uncovered` gains `functions`, the number of functions with a trap of that mnemonic, counted in `coverage()`.\n- `apps/ras/src/report.rs`: `coverage_json` writes that `functions` field.\n- `apps/ras/src/main.rs`: the `--census` flag on `Smoke`, passed into `smoke::Options`.\n- `apps/ras/tests/census.rs` (new): two tests sharing one run over `testdata/bin` (`--census --jobs 4 --out` under the cargo test temp directory).\n  - The first checks the list is sorted and that its trap total equals `coverage.function_traps`.\n  - The second checks each line against its mnemonic in the JSON report (functions, traps, files).\n\nThe last three source files are outside the card's list: the flag has to be declared in `main.rs`, and the per-mnemonic function count did not exist anywhere.\n\n## Things to know\n- **`ras cards --pending` does not accept a census file yet.** The census has a pending list's shape (`#` comments, one entry a line, first word the name), but `Kind::of` in `apps/ras/src/cards.rs` only knows the `testdata` lists and refuses any other file. A census kind needs a file name and a replay command, and `ras smoke <dir> --census` does not fit the `test result: ok` acceptance wording; that is a card of its own.\n- **`--json` wins over `--census`.** With both, stdout is the JSON report, which carries the same counts.\n- **The tests may be thin on `testdata/bin`.** The oracles hold the fixtures at zero traps, so the census there may be empty or nearly so, and both tests then pass on little data. A fixture that traps on purpose would give them substance.\n- **The tests do not assert the exit status of `ras smoke`.** A fixture that panics would fail `tests/smoke.rs`' concern, not the census; they only require that at least one file went through.\n- **Workbench JSON changed, additively.** `coverage.uncovered[].functions` now appears wherever `coverage_json` is used (`ras lift --report --json`, `ras functions --json`), and the smoke report has `coverage.function_traps` and `coverage.uncovered[].functions`. `../rhipper-ide`'s `docs/rhipper/rhipper-asm.md` should follow.\n\n## Verified by reading\n- `Uncovered` is built in one place only, and `smoke::Options` in one place only (`main.rs`).\n- `rustfmt --check` is clean on `smoke.rs`, `census.rs`, `main.rs`, `report.rs` and `lib.rs`; `rustfmt` was run on `smoke.rs` and `census.rs`.\n\n## What the check will prove\n- That it compiles, and that `cargo test -q -p ras --test census` prints `test result: ok` with 2 tests.\n- That the run over about 40 images in `testdata/bin` fits the 60 s per-file limit on a debug build.\n\n## Faster next time\n- The card lists only `smoke.rs`, but the flag lives in `apps/ras/src/main.rs` and the per-mnemonic function count had to be added in `crates/ra-asm-lift/src/lib.rs` and `apps/ras/src/report.rs`; listing them would have saved the search.\n- The card says "a pending list `ras cards` reads", but `cards.rs` refuses unknown list names; one line saying whether a census kind belongs to this card would have settled the scope.\n\n## Commit\n\nAdd `ras smoke --census`: trapped mnemonics by functions affected\n\nThe lift's coverage counts, per mnemonic, the functions with a trap\nof it; the smoke report sums that over the files and `--census`\nprints it as a pending list, the most functions affected first.\nThe report also carries the sum of the functions' trap counts.

          notes: The ABI step now returns its assumptions beside the signatures, with seven tests in the new `assumptions` test file; nothing was compiled or run here, so the check is the first build.\n\n## What changed\n\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/assume.rs`** (new): the record `Assumption { function, step, kind, site }`, with `Step::Abi` and the kinds `CalleeSaved`, `ReturnAddress` and `StackPointer`. `site` is an `Option<u64>` (the call instruction's address), because an IR instruction's address is optional. `assume::sort` orders a list by function, site, step, kind.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/abi.rs`**:\n  - `infer_assuming(..) -> Inferred { signatures, assumptions }` is the inference; `infer_with` now delegates to it, so `infer` and every existing caller are unchanged.\n  - `assumptions(module, signatures, target)` gives the same list for signatures already in hand.\n  - A direct `F_` call or an `ra_call` lists the three kinds at its address. `ReturnAddress` is listed only where the target pushes one, so not on ARM.\n  - A call to an import wrapper or to a callee whose signature is `noreturn` lists nothing, so a leaf's list is empty.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/mod.rs`**: `pub mod assume;` only.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/tests/assumptions.rs`** (new): seven tests, all named `abi_*` so the acceptance filter selects them.\n  - Hand-built machine functions cover two calls giving two callee-saved sites, the kinds per target, a leaf, the import and `noreturn` cases, and order independent of module order.\n  - `control64.exe` covers `fib`'s single call at `0x140001413`, the leaves `collatz` and `op_add`, and two runs giving equal lists.\n\n`raise()` still returns only the signatures, so nothing consumes the assumptions yet; changing its return type touches callers outside this card's files. No JSON changed.\n\n## Verified by reading\n\n- The fixture addresses come from `llvm-objdump` on `testdata/bin/control64.exe` and `control64.map`.\n- Lifted instructions carry their own address: `x86.rs` sets the builder's address to `i.ip()` per instruction.\n- `rustfmt --check` is clean on the three Rust files I wrote or edited.\n\n## What the check will prove\n\n- That it compiles, in particular the `assert_eq!` comparisons of a `Vec` against an array of integer literals in the tests.\n- That the lift emits `fib`'s recursion as a direct `F_1400013f0` call carrying address `0x140001413`; the fixture test rests on this.\n\n## Commit\n\n```\nReturn the ABI step's assumptions beside its signatures\n\nRaising relies on what the machine level does not promise. Each\nreturning call to a lifted function or through ra_call now records\nthat the callee keeps the callee-saved registers, leaves the pushed\nreturn address alone and restores the stack pointer.\n```

          objective: The same for: a local whose address is not taken is not reached by another pointer, pushes and pops balance on every path, the frame pointer is a frame pointer.

          notes: The promote, stack and frame steps now each list what they assumed, with twelve new tests in `assumptions.rs`. Nothing was compiled or run here; only `rustfmt --check` ran, and it is clean on the five files.\n\n## What changed\n\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/assume.rs`** (not in the card's list, but the record lives there): `Step::Promote`, `Step::Stack`, `Step::Frame`, and the kinds `PrivateLocal`, `BalancedStack` and `FramePointer`.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/promote.rs`**: `promote_abi` now returns its assumptions, and `promote::assumptions(f)` gives the same list for a machine function.\n  - It lists `PrivateLocal` at each call into lifted code (`F_` or `ra_call`): the callee reaches the caller's frame only through an address it was handed.\n  - An import lists nothing, and machine mode assumes nothing because it keeps the one stack.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/stack.rs`**: `StackInfo` gains `assumptions`, filled once the dataflow settles.\n  - `BalancedStack` is listed only where balance is not visibly true: a block its paths reach at different known depths, and a return whose depth is unknown or differs from what the signature says.\n  - `FramePointer` is listed at a call into lifted code made while a callee-saved register other than the stack pointer holds a frame address.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/frame.rs`**: `run_assuming(f) -> (bool, Vec<Assumption>)`, with `run` delegating to it.           objective: The analysis assumes an index stays inside its table when no bound check dominates the jump, and devirtualization assumes the candidate set is complete. Both become assumptions with their site; a table whose bound check was found is recorded as discharged.
          acceptance: `cargo test -q -p ra-asm-lift --test assumptions -- tables calls` prints `test result: ok` with at least 3 tests over dispatch64.exe and classes64.exe.
          notes: Jump tables and devirtualized calls now list their assumptions, and a table whose bound check was found is listed as discharged by the compare's address. Nothing was compiled or run here; only `rustfmt --check` ran on the nine files, and it is clean.\n\n## What changed\n\n- **`crates/ra-asm-analysis/src/jumptable.rs`**: `Table` gains `bound: Option<u64>`, the address of the `cmp` that bounds the index. `bound_of` returns the entry count with that address, and all three `resolve` paths carry it.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-analysis/src/function.rs`** and **`builder.rs`** (not in the card's list): `Terminator::Switch` gains `bound`, so the fact reaches the lift. Every existing pattern on it uses `..`.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/src/raise/assume.rs`**:\n  - `Step::JumpTable` and `Step::Devirtualize`, `Kind::TableIndex` and `Kind::CompleteCandidates`.\n  - `Assumption` gains `discharged: Option<u64>` and `is_discharged()`.\n  - `tables(functions)` lists one `TableIndex` per resolved switch, at the jump, discharged by the compare when there is one.\n  - `calls(f, vtables, target, traced, lifted)` lists `CompleteCandidates` at each call `abi::virtual_calls` resolves. A call a traced run resolved lists nothing, because the rewrite keeps the indirect call for other targets.\n- **`abi.rs`, `promote.rs`, `stack.rs`, `frame.rs`** (not in the card's list): their four `Assumption` literals gain `discharged: None`.\n- **`crates/ra-asm-lift/tests/assumptions.rs`**: three `tables_*` and two `calls_*` tests.\n\nThree things to know:\n\n- **Neither named fixture has a jump table.** `dispatch64.exe` and `classes64.exe` only have indirect jumps that do not resolve as tables, so their `tables_*` test asserts an empty list. The discharged and undischarged cases are tested on `classify` in `control64.exe`, the second by clearing its `bound`.\n- **`raise()` still does not collect the lists**, as with the previous card; `mod.rs` is untouched.\n- **JSON:** `functions.json` is unchanged, since `apps/ras/src/functions.rs` builds it by hand. Anything that uses the derived `Serialize` of the analysis `Terminator` gains a `bound` key on `switch`; I did not find such a consumer in `apps/ras/src/*.rs`.\n\n## Verified by reading\n\n- Only `builder.rs` constructs `Terminator::Switch` and only `read_table` constructs `Table`; no other `Assumption` literal exists.\n- `classify` (0x140001430): the `cmp ecx, 8; ja` at 0x140001434 sits in the predecessor block, the index reaches `rax` through `mov eax, ecx`, and the jump is at 0x14000144d.\n- The call sites in `describe` (0x14000107b, 0x140001098, 0x1400010ac) and `describe_named` (0x1400010cb, then the tail jump) come from the disassembly; `dispatch`'s `jmp rax` has no `add` before it, so `resolve` returns `None`.\n- `virtual_calls` and `traced_calls` key on the same `ra_call` argument, so filtering traced calls out by `virtual_candidates` is sound.\n\n## What the check will prove\n\n- That it compiles, in particular `Option<(usize, u64)>::unzip()`, the `impl IntoIterator<Item = &Function>` taking `[&classify]` and `.values().rev()`, and the `[&BTreeMap::new(), &traced]` loop in the tests.\n- `tables_with_a_bound_check_are_listed_discharged`: that the builder has the entry block of `classify` among the predecessors when it resolves the table.\n- `calls_of_classes64_list_the_candidate_sets`: that all three calls of `describe` are keys of `virtual_calls` at the machine level after `simplify::run`. I inferred this from the emit oracle's "no pointer call left" test.\n- `calls_a_run_resolved_assume_no_complete_set`: that `dispatch`'s tail jump lifts as an `ra_call` carrying an address.\n\n## Faster next time\n\n- The card names `dispatch64.exe` for tables, but it has none; naming `control64.exe` (`classify`) as the table fixture would have saved the disassembly search.\n- The card's file list omitted `function.rs` and `builder.rs`, which is how a `Table` fact reaches the lift, and the four steps whose literals a new `Assumption` field touches.\n\n## Commit\n\n```\nList what jump tables and devirtualized calls assume\n\nA resolved table records the compare that bounds its index, and the\nswitch terminator carries it.           notes: ## Report\n\nThe `computed` fixture is written, built for 32 and 64 bit, and both native builds run and print the same four lines. The acceptance test is written but not run here (no cargo builds in this session), and the scoreboard row is not done: no scoreboard exists in this tree yet.\n\n**What changed**\n- `testdata/src/computed.c` (new): `h_blend`, `h_fold` and `h_weigh` are called only through `GetModuleHandleW(NULL) + (masked_offsets[i] - key)`, with `key` a volatile read at run time.\n- `testdata/build.ps1`: a `computed` line (`Build-Exe 'computed' @()`), so `./testdata/build.ps1 computed` rebuilds it.\n- `testdata/bin/computed64.exe`, `computed32.exe` and their `.map` files (new), built with clang 22.1.7.\n- `testdata/expected/computed.txt` (new): `122`, `62`, `40`, `336`, taken from the native runs.\n- `crates/ra-asm-analysis/tests/fixtures.rs`: `the_functions_computed_reaches_by_arithmetic_are_not_found`, the only test the `computed` filter matches. For both builds it asserts that every map function except the three is found, that the three are not, and that the count is the map's minus three.\n- `testdata/README.md`: the fixture's row.\n\n**How the three functions stay hidden**\n- **No reference in the image:** the table is file-scope assembly, `.long h_x@IMGREL+0x5eed0000`, so the linker computes offset plus mask. The words are neither addresses nor offsets, and no base relocation falls in `.rdata`.\n- **No unwind entry on x64:** the functions are leaves marked `nouwtable`; `.pdata` lists only `print_int`, `mainCRTStartup`, `apply` and `resolve`.\n- **No fill before them:** `minsize` drops their alignment, so they sit packed right after `print_int` at unaligned addresses (x64 `0x14000108c`, `…92`, `…9a`; x86 `0x401083`, `…8d`, `…9c`). My first build had them 16-aligned after `nop` fill, where `gap_seeds` in `crates/ra-asm-analysis/src/lib.rs` would have picked them up.\n- **`print_int` is not static:** that makes the compiler emit it ahead of the hidden functions, so they follow a function's last byte rather than the section start.\n\n**Verified by reading:** none of the analysis's seed sources (entry, exports, TLS, `.pdata`, vtables, immediates, data pointers, gap sweep) has a way to these addresses. The check will prove the test passes.\n\n**Open ends**\n- **Scoreboard:** there is no `docs/scoreboard.md`, no `ras score` and no `apps/ras/tests/score.rs` here. The row has to come with the card that adds them.\n- **Oracles:** the fixture is not added to the lift, emit or pipeline oracles; whether its port traps at the computed call was not run here.\n\n## Faster next time\n- The card listed only the four fixture files, but the acceptance needs a test in `crates/ra-asm-analysis/tests/fixtures.rs` and a line in `testdata/build.ps1`; naming them would have saved the search.\n- The card could have said that `.pdata` and the gap sweep (`gap_seeds`) find unreferenced functions; I found the second only after a first build and had to rebuild.\n- [machine] `rg` is not on Git Bash's PATH although the hook allows it; use the Grep tool or `grep`.\n\n## Commit\nAdd computed fixture: functions reached only by computed addresses\n\nThree leaf functions are called through the image base plus an offset\nunmasked at run time; no call, pointer, relocation, unwind entry or\nalignment fill marks them. The analysis test pins that they are not\nfound while every other function of the link map is.

          objective: (generates) hello32, hello64 and control32 packed by upx, committed with the manifest's command, and named by `protector_of`. Their expected output is their unpacked twins'.

          notes: The three packed fixtures are generated and a test names them UPX; nothing was compiled here, so the check is the first run of the test.\n\n- **Fixtures:** `testdata/bin/hello32-upx.exe`, `testdata/bin/hello64-upx.exe` and `testdata/bin/control32-upx.exe` are packed from their twins with upx 5.3.0 (`upx --best -o <name>-upx.exe <name>.exe`). They are untracked and need committing.\n- **Test:** `the_upx_fixtures_are_named_by_their_packer` in `crates/ra-asm-analysis/tests/fixtures.rs` asserts `protector_of` gives `Some("UPX")` for each packed image and `None` for each unpacked twin. It is the only test in that file with `upx` in its name, so the filter selects one.\n- **Rebuild command:** `testdata/build.ps1` has a new `upx` block after the hello and control builds that repacks the three with the same command. It takes upx from the PATH or the winget path, and leaves the committed binaries alone when upx is absent.\n- **Docs:** `testdata/README.md` has a row for the three binaries. This file and `build.ps1` are outside the card's file list.\n\nVerified by running and reading:\n- `llvm-readobj --sections` shows `UPX0`, `UPX1`, `UPX2` in all three, which `protector_by_section` maps to `"UPX"`.\n- The two packed hello programs print `39` and exit 0, as `testdata/expected/hello.txt` has it. `control32-upx.exe` runs and prints control's output; I did not diff it against `expected/control.txt`.\n\nWhat the check will prove: that `ra_asm_pe::load` accepts the packed images. `UPX0` has no raw data, and I did not read the loader for that case.\n\nOne stray empty directory, `var/upx`, was created and can be deleted.\n\n## Faster next time\n- The card says "the manifest's command" but the manifest lists upx as not found; giving the upx path and flags (`--best -o`) in the card would have saved the search.\n- [machine] The hook refuses `& $upx …` and Bash `for` loops with variables; the literal winget path once per file is the only form that runs.\n\n## Commit\nAdd UPX-packed hello and control fixtures and name their packer\n\nhello32, hello64 and control32 packed by upx 5.3.0 --best join\ntestdata/bin; build.ps1 repacks them when upx is present.\nA fixtures test asserts protector_of names the three UPX and\ntheir unpacked twins nothing.
